## My PMF read, as your cofounder
**Yes — and this is the sharpest framing of the product yet.** Three things make me believe it:
1. **It productizes your actual moat.** You already persist candidate rally segments — a *queryable index → stitch* surface is the natural product over the segmentation engine you've spent months sharpening. You're selling your strength, not a generic "AI clipper."
2. **The index is the wedge; language is the door.** The durable value is rally-detection quality (especially multi-court); NL is the friendly front-end that makes it self-serve for a non-technical coach. So: invest in the index, treat the chat as delight — and never let the copy promise a query the index can't answer yet.
3. **It strengthens your GTM loop.** "Ask for your reel" is a far better field-visit demo than "here's an auto reel," *and* every query result is a natural moment for a coach to say "that one's not a rally" — which is exactly the labelled-correction flywheel your whole strategy depends on.

The one discipline I'd hold: keep the alpha's queries to **length / count / order / game** (what the index actually knows) until shot/player detection lands. That keeps the magic honest.

1	# Video Recording Guidelines (DRAFT — thresholds pending empirical data)
2	
3	**Status:** 🚧 Draft / placeholder · **Date:** 2026-05-30
4	**Why this exists:** highlight quality is bounded by *input* quality. To get reliably good auto-generated highlights, the source video must be recorded a certain way (camera height, court position, resolution, frame rate, stability, framing). We want to (1) **derive** the minimum acceptable capture spec **empirically** from the precision/recall of the solutions we build, and (2) **publish user-facing best practices** so a user knows how to record footage that the product can index well.
5	
6	> **This is a tracked thought, not a finished standard.** The exact numeric thresholds below are **placeholders** to be replaced with values measured against our golden set. See "How we'll set the real numbers" (§3). Captured now (per the team's working agreement) so it isn't lost; to be revisited.
7	>
8	> **Update (2026-06-02):** the **frame-rate** and **camera-geometry** rows now carry **literature-backed starting values** (cited inline). They remain *starting points* to confirm empirically per §3. The golden-set **corpus** diversity spec (what footage to collect, not just how users should record) is now in [GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md](GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md).
9	>
10	> **Update (2026-06-07):** **AUDIO is now a first-class capture requirement** (new row below), per ADR-007 — we will use the shuttle "thwack" as a rally cue (audio+visual fusion lifts recall). This is a hard "record with audio on, mic unobstructed" ask, not a placeholder; efficacy on real GoPro audio is being validated (experiment E1 in [AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md](archives/research/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md)).
11	
12	---
13	
14	## ⚠️ THE #1 CAPTURE RULE: ONE MATCH PER FRAME (single court)
15	
16	> **For the best auto-highlights, record a SINGLE match on a SINGLE court — with no other matches
17	> playing in the background or on adjacent courts.** This is the happy path the product is tuned for,
18	> and as of **2026-06-18 it is MEASURED, not a guess:**
19	>
20	> - On a golden **multi-court** clip, the rally detector fired **87 "rallies" against 43 real ones —
21	>   precision ≈ 0.20 (~80% false positives)**, almost all of them background-court games it mistook
22	>   for play.
23	> - On **single-court** clips, precision is markedly higher and rally boundaries are tight; local even
24	>   **out-recalls Gemini** on normal single-match play.
25	>
26	> **Multi-court is a KNOWN BOTTLENECK we are actively working to fix** (player-state + court-occupancy
27	> signals — see [RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md](RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md)). Until that lands,
28	> **single-match capture is strongly recommended** — a reel made from busy multi-court footage *will*
29	> include false "rallies" from the neighbouring games. **North star (for now): consistently beat Gemini
30	> on the single-match happy path.**
31	
32	---
33	
34	## 1. Why input capture matters (grounded in what we already know)
35	
36	ADR-004 found a concrete, capture-dependent failure mode: on **broadcast** footage the camera *tracks the players*, so player pixel-motion dips during rallies and spikes on pans/replays — making player-motion a poor rally proxy. The lesson generalizes: **the pipeline's accuracy is conditional on how the video was shot.** A **static, full-court** camera (typical of a fixed tripod recording) behaves very differently from broadcast — and is likely much friendlier to both the motion/detector stages and the shuttle-tracking substrate (WASB/TrackNet, ADR-001).
37	
38	So capture guidance isn't cosmetic — it directly moves precision/recall.
39	
40	---
41	
42	## 2. The dimensions that likely matter (hypotheses to test)
43	
44	| Dimension | Why it plausibly affects P/R | Placeholder guidance (UNVERIFIED) |
45	|---|---|---|
46	| **Resolution** | Shuttle is tiny & fast; too few pixels → detector misses it | ≥ **1080p** preferred; **720p** likely floor; 2K/4K may help shuttle tracking but ↑ compute/cost |
47	| **Frame rate** | Motion blur; boundary precision; fast-ball detection | ≥ **30 fps** badminton/pickleball; **≥ 60 fps table tennis** — 30 fps collapses fast-ball detection (**22.5% → 93.2%** at 60 fps; 120 ≈ 60, [Nature Sci Rep](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-023-42966-6)) |
48	| **Camera position** | Static full-court view avoids the broadcast camera-tracking trap (ADR-004) | **Fixed tripod**, elevated, behind/above one baseline, whole court in frame |
49	| **Camera height & angle** | Occlusion of players/shuttle; court keystoning | **3–5 m** elevated (~4.5 m), **~30° down-tilt**, behind one baseline, whole court + net headroom ([badminton-CV setup](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10747833/)) |
50	| **Framing / margins** | Cropping the court or players breaks detection & relevance check | Entire court + a margin; players never leave frame |
51	| **Stability** | Handheld jitter inflates motion signals | Tripod / fixed mount; no panning or zoom during play |
52	| **Lighting / exposure** | Blur & contrast affect detection and the existing blur/relevance validators | Even indoor lighting; avoid backlight/glare; minimize motion blur |
53	| **Single match in frame (no background games) — ✅ MEASURED, the #1 rule** | Adjacent/background-court games get detected as false rallies — this is the **dominant precision failure** | **One match per frame, single court.** Multi-court precision measured at **≈0.20** (vs ≈0.31 single, tighter boundaries); multi-court fix in progress — see [RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md](RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md) |
54	| **Court contrast / background** | Relevance check uses court color; busy crowds add false motion | Clear court markings; minimal moving background where possible |
55	| **Codec / container** | Pipeline already validates MP4 + decodes H.264/HEVC | MP4 (H.264/HEVC) as today; align with `archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md` codec notes |
56	| **Audio (REQUIRED — ADR-007)** | Shuttle "thwack" hits are a strong rally cue; audio+visual fusion lifts rally *recall* (the WASB bottleneck) — see ADR-007 / archives/research/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md | **Record with AUDIO ENABLED** (GoPro default — do not mute). Keep the **mic unobstructed**; **avoid aggressive wind/noise reduction** that flattens the sharp impact transient. Camera reasonably close to the court helps SNR (shuttle hits are quiet). |
57	
58	These map onto checks that **already exist** in `backend/pipeline/validators/` (resolution, fps, blur, scene-cut, court relevance) — so several can be enforced/measured programmatically rather than only advised.
59	
60	---
61	
62	## 3. How we'll set the REAL numbers (empirical plan)
63	
64	Replace every placeholder above with a value derived from data, using the golden-set harness:
65	
66	1. Take golden-set clips (GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md) spanning a **range** of each dimension — e.g. the same match re-encoded at 480p/720p/1080p/4K, or clips at different camera heights.
67	2. Run the production pipeline; score with `backend/eval/metrics.py`.
68	3. Plot **precision/recall vs. each dimension** → find the knee where quality collapses.
69	4. Set the **published minimum** at (knee + safety margin); set a **"recommended"** tier above it.
70	5. Re-derive when the pipeline changes materially (new detector/segmenter shifts the knees).
71	
72	This turns "record in 1080p" from an assertion into a measured threshold — and lets us tell users *why*.
73	
74	---
75	
76	## 4. Two surfaces for the output
77	
78	1. **User-facing best-practices guide** — short, friendly, illustrated: "Put the camera here, this high, record at ≥X, keep it still." The goal is that a user can record once and get great results.
79	2. **Programmatic pre-flight check** — extend `backend/pipeline/validators/` to *reject or warn* on out-of-spec uploads at ingest (resolution/fps/blur/framing), so the product fails fast with actionable feedback instead of producing poor highlights silently.
80	
81	---
82	
83	## 5. Open questions
84	1. Which dimension dominates — resolution, camera staticness, or height? (Run the ablations in §3 to rank them.)
85	2. Do we **enforce** minimums (reject uploads) or only **warn**? Likely warn first, enforce once thresholds are trustworthy.
86	3. Single fixed-camera spec, or several supported "recording profiles" (e.g. baseline-elevated vs. side-elevated)?
87	4. How does the owned/licensed golden corpus need to grow to *cover* the capture conditions real users will actually use? (Feeds GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md §8.)
88

### 176. assistant (2026-06-19T18:58:02.956Z)

"""Generate og.png (1200x630) — the social-share card for khelsutra.guru.
Re-run after copy/brand tweaks:  python artifacts/landing/make_og.py
"""
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont

W, H = 1200, 630
INK, INK2 = (12, 38, 31), (20, 62, 47)
GREEN, LIME = (25, 179, 107), (199, 242, 176)
WHITE, MUT = (234, 243, 238), (167, 195, 183)
F = "C:/Windows/Fonts/"

img = Image.new("RGB", (W, H))
d = ImageDraw.Draw(img)

# vertical gradient background
for y in range(H):
    t = y / H
    d.line([(0, y), (W, y)], fill=tuple(int(a + (b - a) * t) for a, b in zip(INK, INK2)))

def font(name, size):
    return ImageFont.truetype(F + name, size)

bold = lambda s: font("segoeuib.ttf", s)
reg = lambda s: font("segoeui.ttf", s)

M = 80  # margin

# --- wordmark + shuttle mark ---
cx, cy = M + 16, 112
d.ellipse([cx - 17, cy - 1, cx + 17, cy + 33], fill=LIME)        # cork
for dx in (-22, -8, 8, 22):
    d.line([(cx, cy), (cx + dx, cy - 46)], fill=LIME, width=5)   # feathers
d.text((M + 52, 92), "Khel", font=bold(40), fill=WHITE)
khel_w = d.textlength("Khel", font=bold(40))
d.text((M + 52 + khel_w, 92), "Sutra", font=bold(40), fill=LIME)

# --- headline ---
d.text((M, 232), "Search your rallies.", font=bold(78), fill=WHITE)
d.text((M, 322), "Build reels by asking.", font=bold(78), fill=LIME)

# --- subline ---
d.text((M, 436), "Turn a full match into searchable rallies — the dead time stripped out.",
       font=reg(30), fill=MUT)

# --- badge pill ---
pill = "Invite-only alpha · Bengaluru"
pf = bold(24)
pw = d.textlength(pill, font=pf)
d.rounded_rectangle([M, 506, M + pw + 44, 552], radius=23, fill=(199, 242, 176, 255),
                    outline=None)
# darker lime text on lime pill
d.text((M + 22, 512), pill, font=pf, fill=(10, 70, 45))

# --- domain bottom-right ---
dom = "khelsutra.guru"
df = bold(28)
dw = d.textlength(dom, font=df)
d.text((W - M - dw, 520), dom, font=df, fill=LIME)

# --- rally-index timeline motif (top-right) ---
bars = [(0, 70, GREEN), (84, 52, (70, 92, 82)), (150, 96, GREEN),
        (262, 44, (70, 92, 82)), (322, 78, GREEN)]
bx, by = W - M - 400, 150
for off, wbar, col in bars:
    d.rounded_rectangle([bx + off, by, bx + off + wbar, by + 16], radius=8, fill=col)

img.save("artifacts/landing/og.png")
print("wrote artifacts/landing/og.png", img.size)

<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8" />
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1" />
<title>KhelSutra — 1-page capture checklist</title>
<meta name="description" content="How to record a badminton session so KhelSutra gives you great highlight reels: camera placement, settings, stability, lighting." />
<link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 32 32'%3E%3Ccircle cx='16' cy='22' r='6' fill='%2319B36B'/%3E%3Cpath d='M16 16 L9 3 M16 16 L16 2 M16 16 L23 3' stroke='%230C261F' stroke-width='2.6' stroke-linecap='round'/%3E%3C/svg%3E" />
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com" />
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.gstatic.com" crossorigin />
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Plus+Jakarta+Sans:wght@400;500;600;700;800&display=swap" rel="stylesheet" />
<style>
  :root{--ink:#0C261F;--green:#19B36B;--green-d:#137a4c;--paper:#F6F8F4;--tx:#14271F;--mut:#566B61;--line:#E1E7DF;--font:'Plus Jakarta Sans',system-ui,-apple-system,Segoe UI,Roboto,sans-serif}
  *{box-sizing:border-box}
  body{margin:0;font-family:var(--font);color:var(--tx);background:#E9EDE6;line-height:1.5;-webkit-font-smoothing:antialiased}
  .sheet{max-width:820px;margin:18px auto;background:#fff;border-radius:16px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:0 18px 50px -24px rgba(0,0,0,.35)}
  .head{background:var(--ink);color:#EAF3EE;padding:22px 30px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-between;gap:1rem;flex-wrap:wrap}
  .brand{display:flex;align-items:center;gap:.55rem;font-weight:800;font-size:1.2rem}
  .brand .dot{color:var(--green)}
  .brand svg{width:26px;height:26px}
  .head h1{font-size:1.05rem;font-weight:600;margin:0;color:#C7F2B0}
  .body{padding:24px 30px 8px}
  .intro{color:var(--mut);margin:0 0 16px;font-size:.98rem}
  .rule{display:flex;gap:.7rem;background:#EAF6EF;border:1px solid #BfE6D2;border-radius:12px;padding:14px 16px;margin-bottom:20px}
  .rule .star{color:var(--green-d);font-size:1.3rem;line-height:1}
  .rule b{color:var(--tx)}
  .rule p{margin:.25rem 0 0;color:var(--mut);font-size:.92rem}
  .grid{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:16px 26px}
  .step h3{display:flex;align-items:center;gap:.5rem;font-size:1rem;font-weight:700;margin:0 0 .4rem}
  .step h3 .n{width:24px;height:24px;border-radius:7px;background:var(--green);color:#04140D;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;font-size:.85rem;flex:none}
  .step ul{margin:0;padding-left:1.1rem}
  .step li{margin:.18rem 0;font-size:.92rem;color:#34453d}
  .step li b{color:var(--tx)}
  .cols{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:0;border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:12px;overflow:hidden;margin:22px 0 8px}
  .cols div{padding:14px 16px}
  .cols .do{background:#EAF6EF}
  .cols .dont{background:#Fbeeeb;border-left:1px solid var(--line)}
  .cols h4{margin:0 0 .5rem;font-size:.92rem}
  .cols .do h4{color:var(--green-d)}
  .cols .dont h4{color:#9a3b22}
  .cols ul{margin:0;padding-left:1.1rem}
  .cols li{font-size:.88rem;margin:.16rem 0;color:#3a4b43}
  .consent{font-size:.86rem;color:var(--mut);border-top:1px solid var(--line);margin-top:6px;padding:14px 0 4px}
  .foot{display:flex;justify-content:space-between;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:.5rem;padding:14px 30px 24px;color:var(--mut);font-size:.85rem}
  .foot a{color:var(--green-d);text-decoration:none}
  @media (max-width:680px){.grid,.cols{grid-template-columns:1fr}.cols .dont{border-left:0;border-top:1px solid var(--line)}}
  @media print{
    body{background:#fff}
    .sheet{box-shadow:none;margin:0;max-width:none;border-radius:0}
    @page{margin:12mm}
    .noprint{display:none}
  }
</style>
</head>
<body>
<div class="sheet">
  <div class="head">
    <span class="brand">
      <svg viewBox="0 0 32 32" fill="none" aria-hidden="true"><circle cx="16" cy="22" r="6" fill="#19B36B"/><path d="M16 16 L9 3 M16 16 L16 2 M16 16 L23 3" stroke="#C7F2B0" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round"/></svg>
      Khel<span class="dot">Sutra</span>
    </span>
    <h1>1-page capture checklist</h1>
  </div>
  <div class="body">
    <p class="intro">Great highlights start with a steady, full-court recording. Five minutes of setup makes a big difference to your reels.</p>

    <div class="rule">
      <span class="star" aria-hidden="true">★</span>
      <div>
        <b>The golden rule: one match, one court, in full frame — and don't move the camera.</b>
        <p>Multi-court footage is the hard case we're still sharpening; a single court in frame gives the best results today.</p>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="grid">
      <div class="step">
        <h3><span class="n">1</span>Place the camera</h3>
        <ul>
          <li>Behind <b>one baseline</b>, elevated — a high tripod, a few metres up, or a balcony.</li>
          <li>Tilt down about <b>30°</b>; keep the <b>whole court</b> in frame with a little margin and net headroom.</li>
          <li>Players should never leave the frame.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
      <div class="step">
        <h3><span class="n">2</span>Settings</h3>
        <ul>
          <li><b>1080p or higher</b>, shot in <b>landscape</b>.</li>
          <li><b>30 fps</b> for badminton/pickleball; <b>60 fps</b> for table tennis.</li>
          <li><b>Audio ON</b> (don't mute) — keep the mic unobstructed, wind/noise reduction off.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
      <div class="step">
        <h3><span class="n">3</span>Keep it still</h3>
        <ul>
          <li>Fixed tripod or mount.</li>
          <li><b>No panning, no zooming</b> during play.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
      <div class="step">
        <h3><span class="n">4</span>Light &amp; record</h3>
        <ul>
          <li>Even indoor lighting; avoid backlight, glare and very dark halls.</li>
          <li>Record the <b>whole session</b> and upload it as-is — KhelSutra does the cutting.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="cols">
      <div class="do">
        <h4>Do</h4>
        <ul>
          <li>Fixed camera, full court, behind the baseline</li>
          <li>1080p+, landscape, audio on</li>
          <li>One match on one court</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
      <div class="dont">
        <h4>Avoid</h4>
        <ul>
          <li>Handheld or phone-in-hand</li>
          <li>Broadcast-style pans &amp; zooming</li>
          <li>Several courts in one frame; very dark halls</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
    </div>

    <p class="consent">Please record adults with their okay. Footage involving minors is never used to improve the tool and is never published.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="foot">
    <span>Questions? <a href="mailto:hello@khelsutra.guru">hello@khelsutra.guru</a> · <a href="https://khelsutra.guru">khelsutra.guru</a></span>
    <span class="noprint">Tip: press Ctrl/Cmd + P to print or save as PDF.</span>
  </div>
</div>
</body>
</html>

### 254. assistant (2026-06-19T19:21:12.361Z)

Grounded the assessment in the actual platform docs (quoting them, so this isn't me riffing) — and the verdict is clean:

## This is half-already-designed, on-thesis, and a real differentiator
The **"bring your own storage"** half is *already in your roadmap*. `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3.1` literally specs a `StorageBackend` registry — **"Backends: local, gdrive, s3, gcs, azure … BYO-storage: the tenant registers their own bucket/Drive; raw video never leaves their account"** — built over `fsspec`/`smart_open`, with the auth posture already chosen (narrow `drive.file` OAuth, IAM AssumeRole + presigned URLs, *never persist long-lived keys*). It's roadmap item **P2**. And your own doc already makes the strategic case: *"none of Veo / Hudl / SwingVision / Spiideo offer true BYO-storage or BYO-compute — 'your video, your storage, your compute' is a genuine differentiator for privacy-sensitive academies."*

You've even **proven the ergonomics on yourselves**: `GOLDEN_DATA_SHARING.md` already runs a OneDrive folder that auto-syncs both ways between your GPU box and dev machine. That's exactly this pattern, in production, internally.

**What's genuinely new is the autosync trigger.** Across all the docs, there is *no* watcher/poller/webhook/"new file → process" component. The shipped async job model (#16) is **pull-on-request** (`POST /api/process → 202`). "ingestion" is even named as a layer but never built. So the work splits cleanly:

| Piece | Status |
|---|---|
| Connect GCS / Drive / OneDrive / S3 / Dropbox | **Designed** (`§3.1` StorageBackend, P2) — not built |
| `list()`/`stat()` primitives a poller needs | **Already in the ABC** |
| Dedup so re-syncs don't reprocess | **Already there** — `video_id` = content MD5 idempotency key |
| **Watch folder → auto-enqueue a job** | **Net-new** — the one real new component |
| Reels land back in the library | Existing flow |

## The honest risks — and the one decision that unblocks everything
**Compute placement is the fork that determines who pays and how private it really is:**
- **Desktop app pulls** (their machine watches their synced folder, processes locally): zero egress through us, no raw video ever touches our infra — the strongest privacy story. **But it's gated on your unsolved desktop P0 spike** (WASB is ~3.4× real-time even on a 2070S; a no-GPU laptop could be hours/clip or infeasible).
- **We pull (hosted):** simplest UX, but we briefly hold their video (which *weakens* the "never leaves your account" headline) and someone eats recurring egress. The clean middle is your own `§3.2` **"zero-infra BYO"** — storage stays in their bucket via signed URLs, compute on an ephemeral per-tenant GPU sandbox.

Pick that, and the rest falls out. The other live ones:
- **Egress / "surprise bill":** pulling 1–4 GB per match *out* of their cloud meters egress on *their* provider. Your cost story ("we don't store giant videos") holds for storage but **not automatically for egress** on a we-pull design. Locality discipline says pull a *proxy*, not the 10 GB original — which really wants a client (desktop).
- **OAuth reality:** the privacy-minimal `drive.file` scope only sees files *the app created*. Watching a user's *existing* footage folder needs `drive.readonly` (broader, higher-trust) + **Google/Microsoft/Dropbox app-verification/security review** + refresh-token custody. MS365 needs admin consent. That's a real weeks-long compliance gate, not a weekend.
- **"Stream it, never download" isn't fully true:** detection can stream a proxy, but **stitch reads the original**, and ffmpeg/cv2 want a local file — so originals (or ranged streams) come down at reel time. Be honest in the copy.
- **Autosync hygiene** (the new component's actual work): a watched folder is a firehose — in-flight partial uploads, non-video, HEVC/GoPro-chaptered clips, non-badminton footage, **and your own reels written back to the same bucket** (re-ingest loop). Needs a file-ready check, relevance/codec filtering, and the MD5 dedup ledger. Plus per-provider change-feeds all differ (GCS Pub/Sub, S3 events, Drive push channels that *expire*, Graph delta) — `fsspec` abstracts reads, not change events.
- **Consent gets *bigger*:** a background daemon that auto-processes "whatever lands" removes the human checkpoint → consent-at-capture, minors-exclusion, retention deadline, and "free tier never hits Gemini" become mandatory, not optional.

## Persona nuance worth locking
Your locality doc explicitly calls "expecting academies to manage S3/GCS buckets" a **non-starter**. So split it: **consumer cloud (Drive/OneDrive/Dropbox) → "watch this folder, reels appear"** for the non-technical academy/prosumer; **raw S3/GCS bucket BYO** for the technical/B2B tenant. Same engine, two front doors.

## My recommendation
**Strong yes as a direction — but it's a platform layer, not the next thing.** It sits *after* the immediate launch (tonight's page, the alpha web app, corpus growth) and rides on the **desktop P0 compute spike**, because the make-or-break question (where compute runs / who pays egress) is the same unknown that spike resolves. Beautifully, it composes with the new positioning: **autosync → index rallies → (coming) ask for your reel** = a zero-touch funnel.

When you're ready, I'd capture it as a tracked design doc (it spans three existing ones): connector half stays in `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE §3.1` (P2), and I'd add a new **"IngestTrigger / Watcher"** seam there + push the egress/OAuth/retention open-questions into `VIDEO_LOCALITY_MODEL`'s ledger — it's effectively the autosync front-door to `STORAGE_SHARING_MODEL §7d` (your co-design item #15). I won't start it unprompted (owner-gated strategy), but say the word and I'll draft the doc with the compute-placement decision teed up as the lead question.

For tonight, though — this changes nothing about go-live. Page's ready when you are. 🚀

The Pages error is just **leftover GoDaddy "parking" records** that Cloudflare auto-imported when you added the domain. There's an `A`/`CNAME` record sitting on `khelsutra.guru` (pointing at GoDaddy's parking page), and Pages won't overwrite it. Delete those and retry — 2 minutes.

## Fix: clear the parking records, then re-add the domain
1. Cloudflare dash → click the **`khelsutra.guru`** domain → left sidebar **DNS → Records**.
2. You'll see a few auto-imported records. **Delete** these:
   - The **`A`** record with **Name `khelsutra.guru`** (or `@`) — that's the parking IP.
   - Any **`A`/`CNAME`** with **Name `www`** — parking too.
   - (Safe to also delete any stray parking `CNAME`/`TXT`. **Leave** the `NS` records and a `_domainconnect` CNAME if present — those are harmless.)
   - Since this is a freshly-parked domain, **all existing records are GoDaddy junk** — nothing of yours to lose.

4. Repeat for **`www.khelsutra.guru`** (or set a redirect — your call).

After you delete the A record, the apex briefly has no record — that's expected; Pages recreates the right one. SSL can take a few minutes to go green.

### 303. assistant (2026-06-20T02:57:26.051Z)

Checked the live cert and redirect behavior. **The encryption itself is solid — but there's one real gap a security scanner *will* flag.** Here's the honest picture:

**✅ Certificate is correct and trusted:**
- Issued by **Google Trust Services** (Cloudflare's Universal SSL CA) — publicly trusted, no warnings.
- Covers **`khelsutra.guru` *and* `*.khelsutra.guru`** (SAN), so `www` is covered by the wildcard too (and we already saw `www` serve over HTTPS fine).
- Valid **Jun 20 → Sep 18 2026**, auto-renewing. HTTPS to the site validates cleanly (no chain errors).

**⚠️ The thing that gets flagged — no forced HTTPS:**
- `http://khelsutra.guru` currently returns **200 over plain HTTP** — it does **not** redirect to HTTPS. So the page is reachable insecurely, and browsers/scanners will flag "available over HTTP / not secure."
- There's also **no HSTS header**, which security audits commonly flag.

**Fix — 2 toggles in Cloudflare (1 minute), no redeploy:**
Cloudflare dash → **`khelsutra.guru`** → **SSL/TLS → Edge Certificates**:
1. **"Always Use HTTPS" → ON.** This makes `http://` 301-redirect to `https://`. *This is the one that closes the main flag.*
2. **"HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)" → Enable** → max-age ~6 months. (One caveat: HSTS is sticky — once set, browsers force HTTPS for the domain — but for a static site that's always on HTTPS, it's exactly what you want.)
3. *(Optional hygiene)* same page → **"Minimum TLS Version" → 1.2**.

Also worth a glance: **SSL/TLS → Overview → encryption mode** should read **Full (strict)** — though for a Workers/Pages deployment the origin *is* Cloudflare, so this is largely automatic; just make sure it's **not** set to "Flexible."

Net: your data-in-transit is genuinely protected (valid TLS cert), but flip **"Always Use HTTPS" ON** so nothing's reachable over plain HTTP — that's the toggle that keeps a security scanner from marking it "unprotected." Want me to re-verify the http→https redirect once you've flipped it?

### 331. assistant (2026-06-20T03:09:06.108Z)

Sure — here's the full picture on the **email redirect** (Cloudflare calls it **Email Routing**), which is what makes your `hello@khelsutra.guru` "Request access" button actually work.

## What it is
It's **free email forwarding**. You don't get a new inbox to log into — instead, anything sent to `hello@khelsutra.guru` is **automatically forwarded to an inbox you already use** (your Gmail). So when someone clicks "Request access" on the site, the email lands in your normal Gmail, and you reply from there.

- It's **receive-only forwarding** — think of it as a mail-forwarding sticker on a PO box, not a new mailbox.
- It's **free**, runs entirely on Cloudflare (works because your domain's DNS is now on Cloudflare).
- You can have many addresses: `hello@`, `avi@`, `support@` — all forwarding wherever you want.

## How to set it up (~2 min, no code)
Cloudflare dashboard → click the **`khelsutra.guru`** domain → left sidebar **Email → Email Routing** → **Get started / Enable**, then:
1. **Add a destination address** = your Gmail (e.g. `avi.dullu@gmail.com`). Cloudflare sends a **verification email** to that Gmail — open it and click the confirm link. (One-time; proves you own the inbox.)
2. **Create a custom address**: `hello` @ `khelsutra.guru` → **Action: Send to** → pick your verified Gmail → **Save**.
3. *(Optional)* Toggle **Catch-all** so *anything*@khelsutra.guru (typos, future names) also forwards to you.

Cloudflare then **auto-adds the DNS records** it needs — three **MX** records (`*.mx.cloudflare.net`) and an **SPF TXT** record. You don't touch those; it does it for you. Since khelsutra.guru is a fresh domain with no existing email, there's nothing to conflict with.

That's it — test it by emailing `hello@khelsutra.guru` from any account; it should appear in your Gmail within seconds.

## The one honest caveat: receiving ≠ sending
Email Routing **forwards inbound mail only.** When you reply, by default it goes **from your Gmail address**, not from `hello@khelsutra.guru`. For an invite-only alpha that's totally fine — you'll just reply personally.

If later you want replies to **appear as `hello@khelsutra.guru`**, that's a separate step (Gmail "Send mail as" needs an outbound SMTP relay, which Cloudflare Routing doesn't provide — you'd add a small sender service like a transactional email provider). Not worth it now; flag it for beta.

## Why it matters right now
Your landing page's button is `mailto:hello@khelsutra.guru?subject=KhelSutra alpha access…`. Until Email Routing is set up, **that address doesn't exist** and those emails bounce. Once you do the 3 steps above, every "Request access" click quietly lands in your inbox. ✅

Want me to verify it's working once you've set it up? I can send a quick check or confirm the MX/SPF records resolved correctly.

> **Relation:** first tracked work item in the `khelsutra` repo; mirrors the engine repo's
> `PROJECT_DOC_TEMPLATE` discipline (status header · decisions-locked · §6 tracker · DoD).
> Feeds the platform direction in the engine repo's `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3` (StorageBackend /
> ComputeBackend) and `VIDEO_LOCALITY_MODEL.md` — see §7.

---

## 0. TL;DR
Replace the landing page's `mailto:` "Request access" CTA with a real form that (a) segments
**academies vs individual enthusiasts**, (b) asks the two questions that actually decide how we'd
serve a user — **"do you have a GPU machine?"** (compute placement) and **"where do your videos
live?"** (storage backend) — and (c) keeps a free-text message. Submissions are stored in our own
**Cloudflare D1** via a small **Worker** (`POST /api/request-access`). The form doubles as PMF +
architecture research.

## 1. Problem & goal
Today the CTA is `mailto:hello@khelsutra.guru` — zero structure, nothing collected, no segmentation.
We want **structured, queryable** interest signals from two distinct audiences, captured the moment
someone raises a hand, **without shipping lead data to a third party** (privacy-first ethos).
**Goal:** a clean on-brand form whose responses land in our own Cloudflare account, queryable later.

## 2. Decisions locked
- **Backend = Cloudflare Worker + D1** (owner decision, 2026-06-20). Data stays in our Cloudflare
  account; queryable; no third party. (Alternatives considered: hosted form (Tally/Formspree —
  third-party data), Worker→email-only (no DB), structured `mailto` (no collection). Rejected for
  the reasons in parens.)
- **Two audience segments:** `academy` and `individual` — the form lightly adapts (academy also
  asks club name + #courts).
- **The two discovery questions are first-class** (not optional polish): GPU/compute + storage —
  they map 1:1 onto the BYO-compute / BYO-storage architecture forks (§7).
- **Free-text message retained** ("Anything else?").
- **Privacy:** store only what's submitted + a coarse country (from `request.cf.country`); **no raw
  IP**; consent checkbox for "email me about the alpha"; this data is **operations/contact only**,
  never a training source (same guardrail as the engine repo).
- **Deploy model changes:** the site moves from dashboard direct-upload → **Wrangler** (Worker with
  static assets + D1 binding). Documented in §5; owner runs the `wrangler` deploy (our account/auth).

## 3. Form spec
| Field | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Segment | radio | `academy` \| `individual` (required) |
| Name | text | required |
| Email | email | required (so we can reply) |
| City | text | optional |
| Club / academy name | text | shown for `academy` |
| Courts recorded | select | `1` \| `2-4` \| `5+` (academy; optional) |
| **GPU machine for local processing?** | radio | `yes` \| `unsure` \| `no` \| `prefer-cloud` |
| **Where do your recordings live / how would you share them?** | checkboxes (multi) | `gdrive` \| `onedrive` \| `dropbox` \| `bucket` (S3/GCS) \| `local` (SD/drive) \| `unsure` |
| Message | textarea | free text ("Anything else?") |
| Consent | checkbox | "Okay to email me about the KhelSutra alpha" |
| `hp` | hidden honeypot | anti-spam; if filled → silently drop |

## 4. Design / architecture
- **Frontend** (`site/index.html`): the header + hero "Request access" buttons scroll to the
  `#join` form; `#join` becomes the form. On submit, JS `POST`s JSON to `/api/request-access`.
  **Progressive enhancement:** if the endpoint isn't live yet (pre-Worker), it falls back to a
  prefilled `mailto:hello@` so the form is useful from the first re-upload.
- **Worker** (`/api/request-access`): validates (email present, honeypot empty), inserts into D1,
  returns `200 {ok:true}`; serves the static assets for all other routes.
- **D1 schema** (`access_requests`): `id, created_at, segment, name, email, city, org_name, courts,
  has_gpu, storage (csv), message, consent, country, user_agent`.
- **Anti-spam:** honeypot first; add **Cloudflare Turnstile** in P2 if spam appears.
- **Notify (P2):** optional email ping to `hello@` per submission (MailChannels / Cloudflare Email).

## 5. Deploy (owner-run; our Cloudflare account)
```
wrangler d1 create khelsutra-access            # once; copy the database_id into wrangler.toml
wrangler d1 execute khelsutra-access --file=site/schema.sql   # create the table
wrangler deploy                                # ships the Worker + static assets + D1 binding
```
> Until the Worker ships, the form works via the `mailto` fallback (re-upload `site/` as today).
> Git↔Cloudflare auto-deploy still intentionally not connected.

## 6. Deliverables & progress tracker  ⟵ source of truth
Legend: ☐ Todo · ◐ In progress · ☑ Done. One PR per row off `origin/master`.
| ID | Deliverable | Gated? | Status | PR |
|----|-------------|--------|--------|----|
| P0 | This design doc + `NEXT_STEPS.md` | No | ◐ | this PR |
| P1 | Form UI in `site/index.html` (segments + GPU + storage + free text + consent) w/ `mailto` fallback | No | ◐ | this PR |
| P2 | Worker + D1 + `wrangler.toml` + `schema.sql` (real collection) | owner deploy | ☐ | — |
| P3 | Anti-spam (Turnstile) + per-submission email notify | No | ☐ | — |
| P4 | Tiny admin/export view (auth'd) to read responses | owner | ☐ | — |

## 7. Why this matters beyond lead capture
The two questions are **the architecture forks** from the engine repo's BYO analysis:
- **GPU?** → compute placement: desktop/local (their GPU) vs hosted vs zero-infra-BYO
  (`PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2`, `DESKTOP_APP_PLAN.md` P0 spike).
- **Storage?** → which `StorageBackend` + whether autosync is worth building
  (`PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3.1`, the cloud-storage/autosync thread).
So early responses directly inform what to build next — not just who to email.

## 8. Open questions
1. Turnstile now or wait for spam? (Default: honeypot now, Turnstile if needed.)
2. Email-notify per submission, or just query D1 weekly? (Default: notify in P2.)
3. Do we localize the form (Kannada/Hindi) for academies now or at beta? (Default: English now.)

## 9. Definition of done
- [ ] Form replaces the `mailto` CTA, segments academy/individual, asks GPU + storage + free text.
- [ ] Submissions persist to Cloudflare D1 via the Worker; honeypot drops bots.
- [ ] Privacy posture honored (no raw IP; consent; contact-only).
- [ ] Deployed live on khelsutra.guru; a test submission appears in D1.
- [ ] Doc tracker + DoD updated; archived when P1–P3 land.

# Next steps (live) — KhelSutra brand / site

The prioritized "what to do next" for the **`khelsutra` product repo** (brand site now, SPA + control
plane later). Mirrors the engine repo's `docs/NEXT_STEPS.md` discipline. Refresh at each checkpoint.

**Where we are (2026-06-20):** `khelsutra.guru` is **LIVE** — static marketing landing page +
`/capture` checklist + OG image, on Cloudflare (Workers static-assets, direct upload; custom domain
+ free SSL + email routing for `hello@`). Source in `site/` (PR #1). Git↔Cloudflare auto-deploy
intentionally **not** connected — publish by re-uploading `site/` in the dashboard.

## In progress
- **Request-Access form** — segmented (academy / individual) + GPU + storage discovery questions +
  free text, collected in **Cloudflare D1** via a Worker. Tracked: [`docs/REQUEST_ACCESS_FORM.md`](docs/REQUEST_ACCESS_FORM.md).
  - P1 form UI (this PR) → P2 Worker + D1 (owner-run `wrangler` deploy) → P3 anti-spam + email notify.

## Queued (site)
- **Always-Use-HTTPS + HSTS** toggles in Cloudflare (close the plain-HTTP security flag).
- **Cloudflare Web Analytics** — paste the token into `site/index.html` (or enable automatic setup).
- **`/capture` link tidy** — footer points to `/capture.html` (307→`/capture`); change to `/capture`.
- **OG image refresh** when copy/brand changes (`python site/make_og.py`).
- **Social/DMARC** — add `_dmarc` TXT once we start sending mail (deliverability).
- **Connect git auto-deploy** (deferred by owner) — Cloudflare Pages/Workers Build pointed at `site/`.
- **`send mail as hello@`** for replies (needs an outbound SMTP relay) — beta.

## Bigger arcs (not started; design-first per the engine repo's convention)
- **Alpha SPA** lands in `web/` (Vite + React + TS + Tailwind) per `UI_ALPHA_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` (engine repo).
- **BYO-storage + autosync** — connect Drive/OneDrive/GCS/S3, watch the folder, auto-prepare
  highlights (engine repo `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3` + the autosync thread). The Request-Access
  GPU/storage answers are the demand signal for this.
- **Control plane** (`control-plane/`) at beta.

## Convention
Substantial work gets a tracked doc in `docs/` (template as in the engine repo); one PR per item off
`origin/master`; don't connect prod to git auto-deploy without owner say-so.

## Should you link GitHub ↔ Cloudflare? **Yes — it's both quicker *and* safer.**
The wins are exactly what you'd want:
- **Quick:** `git push` / merge → Cloudflare builds + deploys automatically. No more manual drag-upload.
- **Safe — three ways:**
  - **Versioned rollback** — every deploy is tied to a commit; one click to roll back to any previous deployment.
  - **PR preview URLs** — each PR gets its own live preview link, so you (and I) can *see* a change rendered before it touches production. That's a real upgrade to the review discipline you asked for.
  - **No drift** — the repo becomes the single source of truth; "what's in git" == "what's live." (Literally the trap we just hit when I copied to the wrong folder — git-deploy eliminates it.)

## The timing is perfect — pair it with the Worker (P2)
Here's the one nuance: Cloudflare deploys a Worker from a **`wrangler.toml`** in the repo (via "Workers Builds"). We're *about* to add that anyway for the form's D1 backend. So the clean sequence is:

1. **I build P2** — `wrangler.toml` + the Worker (`/api/request-access` → D1) + `schema.sql`, in a PR. This makes the repo "connect-ready."
2. **You connect the repo** in Cloudflare (Workers Builds → pick `avidullu/khelsutra`, root dir `site/`, bind D1). One-time, ~5 min — I'll give exact click-by-click steps.
3. From then on: **merge to `master` → auto-deploys to khelsutra.guru; PRs → preview URLs.**

**One behavior change to know (it's the "safe" part):** production only moves when you merge to `master`. Feature branches/PRs deploy to *preview*, not production — so nothing goes live by accident.

A couple of setup details we'll handle during the connect: re-attaching the `khelsutra.guru` custom domain to the git-deployed Worker, and the D1 binding. All straightforward; I'll walk it.

### 397. assistant (2026-06-20T05:55:40.716Z)

// Cloudflare Worker for khelsutra.guru — serves the static site and handles the
// Request-Access form: POST /api/request-access -> validate -> insert into D1 (binding DB).
// Pure helpers are exported so they can be unit-tested without a live Worker/D1.

const SEGMENTS = ["academy", "individual"];
const GPU = ["yes", "unsure", "no", "prefer-cloud"];
const COURTS = ["1", "2-4", "5+"];
const STORAGE = ["gdrive", "onedrive", "dropbox", "bucket", "local", "unsure"];

function str(v, max) {
  if (v == null) return null;
  const s = String(v).trim();
  return s ? s.slice(0, max) : null;
}

// Honeypot: humans never fill the hidden "company" field.
export function isBot(p) {
  return !!(p && typeof p.company === "string" && p.company.trim() !== "");
}

export function validate(p) {
  if (!p || typeof p !== "object") return { ok: false, error: "bad payload" };
  const email = String(p.email || "").trim();
  if (!/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/.test(email)) return { ok: false, error: "valid email required" };
  return { ok: true };
}

export function toRow(p, meta = {}) {
  const storage = Array.isArray(p.storage)
    ? p.storage.filter((s) => STORAGE.includes(s)).join(",")
    : "";
  return {
    segment: SEGMENTS.includes(p.segment) ? p.segment : null,
    name: str(p.name, 200),
    email: str(p.email, 320),
    city: str(p.city, 200),
    org_name: str(p.org_name, 200),
    courts: COURTS.includes(p.courts) ? p.courts : null,
    has_gpu: GPU.includes(p.has_gpu) ? p.has_gpu : null,
    storage,
    message: str(p.message, 4000),
    consent: p.consent ? 1 : 0,
    country: str(meta.country, 8),
    user_agent: str(meta.userAgent, 400),
  };
}

function json(obj, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(obj), { status, headers: { "content-type": "application/json" } });
}

export async function handleRequest(request, env) {
  let body;
  try {
    body = await request.json();
  } catch {
    return json({ ok: false, error: "invalid json" }, 400);
  }
  if (isBot(body)) return json({ ok: true }); // silently drop bots
  const v = validate(body);
  if (!v.ok) return json({ ok: false, error: v.error }, 400);

  const r = toRow(body, {
    country: request.headers.get("cf-ipcountry"),
    userAgent: request.headers.get("user-agent"),
  });

  await env.DB.prepare(
    `INSERT INTO access_requests
       (segment, name, email, city, org_name, courts, has_gpu, storage, message, consent, country, user_agent)
     VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`
  )
    .bind(r.segment, r.name, r.email, r.city, r.org_name, r.courts, r.has_gpu, r.storage, r.message, r.consent, r.country, r.user_agent)
    .run();

  return json({ ok: true });
}

export default {
  async fetch(request, env) {
    const url = new URL(request.url);
    if (url.pathname === "/api/request-access") {
      if (request.method !== "POST") return json({ ok: false, error: "method not allowed" }, 405);
      return handleRequest(request, env);
    }
    return env.ASSETS.fetch(request); // everything else: static assets
  },
};

// Unit tests for the Request-Access Worker logic. Run: node --test src/index.test.js
import { test } from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import worker, { validate, toRow, isBot, handleRequest } from "./index.js";

function fakeDB() {
  const inserts = [];
  return {
    inserts,
    prepare(sql) {
      return {
        bind(...args) {
          return { async run() { inserts.push({ sql, args }); return { success: true }; } };
        },
      };
    },
  };
}

function req(payload, headers = {}) {
  return new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json", ...headers },
    body: JSON.stringify(payload),
  });
}

test("validate requires a well-formed email", () => {
  assert.equal(validate({}).ok, false);
  assert.equal(validate({ email: "nope" }).ok, false);
  assert.equal(validate({ email: "a@b.co" }).ok, true);
});

test("isBot detects a filled honeypot only", () => {
  assert.equal(isBot({}), false);
  assert.equal(isBot({ company: "" }), false);
  assert.equal(isBot({ company: "spam" }), true);
});

test("toRow maps + sanitizes a valid payload", () => {
  const r = toRow(
    { segment: "academy", email: "a@b.co", storage: ["gdrive", "bucket", "evil"], has_gpu: "yes", courts: "2-4", consent: true },
    { country: "IN", userAgent: "x" }
  );
  assert.equal(r.segment, "academy");
  assert.equal(r.storage, "gdrive,bucket"); // unknown "evil" dropped
  assert.equal(r.has_gpu, "yes");
  assert.equal(r.courts, "2-4");
  assert.equal(r.consent, 1);
  assert.equal(r.country, "IN");
});

test("toRow rejects invalid enum values", () => {
  const r = toRow({ segment: "hacker", has_gpu: "maybe", courts: "9", consent: 0 });
  assert.equal(r.segment, null);
  assert.equal(r.has_gpu, null);
  assert.equal(r.courts, null);
  assert.equal(r.consent, 0);
  assert.equal(r.storage, "");
});

test("handleRequest inserts a valid submission", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "a@b.co", segment: "individual", storage: ["gdrive"] }), { DB: db });
  assert.equal(res.status, 200);
  assert.deepEqual(await res.json(), { ok: true });
  assert.equal(db.inserts.length, 1);
  assert.equal(db.inserts[0].args[2], "a@b.co"); // email is the 3rd bound column
});

test("handleRequest drops a bot without inserting", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "a@b.co", company: "spam" }), { DB: db });
  assert.equal(res.status, 200);
  assert.equal(db.inserts.length, 0);
});

test("handleRequest rejects a bad email with 400", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "nope" }), { DB: db });
  assert.equal(res.status, 400);
  assert.equal(db.inserts.length, 0);
});

test("handleRequest rejects invalid JSON with 400", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  const bad = new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access", {
    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: "{not json",
  });
  const res = await handleRequest(bad, { DB: db });
  assert.equal(res.status, 400);
  assert.equal(db.inserts.length, 0);
});

test("fetch routes: POST /api hits the handler, other paths delegate to ASSETS", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  const apiRes = await worker.fetch(req({ email: "a@b.co" }), { DB: db });
  assert.equal(apiRes.status, 200);

  let assetCalled = false;
  const env = { DB: db, ASSETS: { fetch: async () => { assetCalled = true; return new Response("ok"); } } };
  const assetRes = await worker.fetch(new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/"), env);
  assert.equal(assetCalled, true);
  assert.equal(await assetRes.text(), "ok");
});

test("fetch rejects non-POST to the API with 405", async () => {
  const res = await worker.fetch(new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access"), { DB: fakeDB() });
  assert.equal(res.status, 405);
});

# Cloudflare Worker for khelsutra.guru — static site + Request-Access form API.
#
# One-time setup (owner, our Cloudflare account):
#   npx wrangler d1 create khelsutra-access          # copy the printed database_id below

#
name = "khelsutra-site"
main = "src/index.js"
compatibility_date = "2025-06-01"

[assets]
directory = "./public"
binding = "ASSETS"

[[d1_databases]]
binding = "DB"
database_name = "khelsutra-access"
database_id = "REPLACE_WITH_D1_DATABASE_ID"   # from `wrangler d1 create khelsutra-access`

-- D1 schema for Request-Access submissions.
-- Apply: npx wrangler d1 execute khelsutra-access --remote --file=schema.sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS access_requests (
  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
  segment    TEXT,            -- 'academy' | 'individual'
  name       TEXT,
  email      TEXT NOT NULL,
  city       TEXT,
  org_name   TEXT,            -- academy / club name
  courts     TEXT,            -- '1' | '2-4' | '5+'
  has_gpu    TEXT,            -- 'yes' | 'unsure' | 'no' | 'prefer-cloud'
  storage    TEXT,            -- csv of gdrive,onedrive,dropbox,bucket,local,unsure
  message    TEXT,            -- free text
  consent    INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
  country    TEXT,            -- coarse, from cf-ipcountry (no raw IP stored)
  user_agent TEXT
);

### 417. assistant (2026-06-20T05:58:10.668Z)

# site/ — public marketing site + Request-Access API (Cloudflare Worker)

The public site at **[khelsutra.guru](https://khelsutra.guru)** — a static landing page +
`/capture` checklist — plus a small Worker that backs the Request-Access form with **Cloudflare D1**.

## Layout
- `public/` — static assets served as-is (`index.html`, `capture.html`, `og.png`).
- `src/index.js` — the Worker: serves `public/` and handles `POST /api/request-access` → D1.
- `src/index.test.js` — unit tests for the form logic.
- `wrangler.toml` — Worker + static-assets + D1 binding config.
- `schema.sql` — the `access_requests` D1 table.
- `make_og.py` — regenerates `public/og.png` (Pillow + Windows fonts): `python make_og.py`.

## Develop / test
```
npm test       # node --test src/  (no install needed)
npm run dev    # npx wrangler dev — local Worker + D1
```

## Deploy (owner — our Cloudflare account)
One-time:
```
npx wrangler d1 create khelsutra-access                          # paste database_id into wrangler.toml
npx wrangler d1 execute khelsutra-access --remote --file=schema.sql
```
Then either:
- **Manual:** `npx wrangler deploy`, or
- **Git (recommended):** connect this repo in Cloudflare → Workers Builds (root dir `site/`), bind the
  D1 database → every merge to `master` deploys; PRs get preview URLs.

## Notes
- The form posts to `/api/request-access`; if the Worker is unreachable it falls back to a prefilled
  `mailto:hello@`, so it degrades gracefully.
- `hello@khelsutra.guru` relies on Cloudflare Email Routing.
- The Web Analytics beacon in `public/index.html` has a placeholder token (`REPLACE_WITH_CF_TOKEN`)
  — paste the real token or enable Cloudflare automatic setup.

Everything is **in `master` and tested**, but **nothing changed on the live site yet.** khelsutra.guru still serves the original (form-less) page, because the Worker hasn't been *deployed* — that's an owner step on your Cloudflare account.

**What I verified:** the form (rendering + academy-toggle, in-browser) and the Worker logic (10 unit tests: validation, honeypot, sanitization, D1 insert, routing, 400/405). **What only the deploy can prove:** the live D1 round-trip — that's confirmed the moment you ship.

## To actually go live (owner steps — I'll co-pilot like the first launch)
This is the same session where we connect git, as you decided:
1. **Create the database:** `npx wrangler d1 create khelsutra-access` → it prints a `database_id`.
2. **Wire it:** paste that id into `site/wrangler.toml` (it's not a secret — safe to commit), then apply the schema: `npx wrangler d1 execute khelsutra-access --remote --file=site/schema.sql`.
3. **Connect git:** Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → **Create → Workers → Connect to Git** → `avidullu/khelsutra`, **root directory `site/`**, bind the D1 database → it deploys the `khelsutra-site` Worker. (From then on: merge to `master` → deploys; PRs → preview URLs.)
4. **Cutover the domain** (the one gotcha): khelsutra.guru is currently attached to the *old* auto-named Worker (`hidden-king-khelsutra`). Move the custom domain (`khelsutra.guru` + `www`) onto the new `khelsutra-site` Worker, then delete the old one.

After that: submitting the form writes a row to D1, and you can `wrangler d1 execute … "SELECT * FROM access_requests"` to read responses — including those GPU/storage answers that tell you what to build next.

**Want to do this deploy + git-connect now**, the same way we did the first launch (you click, I walk each step)? Or park it for later — the repo's ready whenever you are. Either way: form + backend built, **10/10 tests green, both PRs merged.** 🚀

### 434. user (2026-06-20T06:03:17.885Z)

Based on the pricing documentation, the Cloudflare D1 FREE plan includes these limits:

- **Rows read**: "5 million / day"
- **Rows written**: "100,000 / day"
- **Storage**: "5 GB (total)"

These allowances are tied to the Workers Free plan and reset daily at 00:00 UTC. Once exceeded, the D1 API returns errors preventing further queries until the next day's reset.

### 435. user (2026-06-20T06:03:18.917Z)

# Cloudflare Workers Free Plan Limits

Based on the pricing documentation, here are the specific free-tier limitations:

**Requests:** The free plan permits "100,000 per day" of inbound requests to your Worker.

**CPU Time:** You receive "10 milliseconds of CPU time per invocation" at no charge, with no duration limit otherwise.

**Static Assets:** Excellent news for static content—"Requests to static assets are free and unlimited," meaning they don't count against your request quota and incur no charges.

Additionally, the free plan includes other complimentary services like Workers Logs (200,000 log events daily with 3-day retention), Workers KV access, and Hyperdrive database queries (100,000 per day).

### 436. assistant (2026-06-20T06:03:48.603Z)

Confirmed from Cloudflare's pricing docs — **no, this stays comfortably in the free tier**, by a wide margin. And critically: on the free plan, exceeding a limit returns errors (rate-limits you), it **does not auto-bill** — so there's no surprise-charge risk, and no card needed.

Here's the math against your actual (invite-only alpha) usage:

| Component | Free-tier limit | Your usage | Headroom |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Workers — static serving** | **Free & unlimited** (doesn't count) | All landing-page traffic | ∞ |
| **Workers — dynamic requests** | 100,000 / **day** | 1 per form submit → dozens? | ~99.9% unused |
| **D1 — rows written** | 100,000 / **day** | 1 per submit | microscopic |
| **D1 — rows read** | 5,000,000 / **day** | your occasional `SELECT` | microscopic |
| **D1 — storage** | 5 GB | ~1 KB/row → ~5M submissions to fill | never |
| **Email Routing · SSL · DNS · Analytics** | Free | — | — |

The key insight from the docs: **serving the site is free and uncounted** — only the *form POSTs* hit the Worker, and you'd need **100,000 submissions in a single day** to touch the limit. Git-connected builds also have a generous free monthly allotment, far above your commit cadence.

**So you'd only ever cross into paid if you deliberately opt into the Workers Paid plan ($5/mo)** — which you'd do for *scale* (millions of requests) or extras (Durable Objects, higher CPU), none of which the landing page + alpha form needs.

**One honest framing, so the cost picture is clear:** the real money in this product lives in **video processing** — GPU compute, the Gemini API, and video storage/egress (the stuff we cost-analyzed in the engine repo). That's a separate system. **The marketing site + lead form is, and will stay, free.** The BYO-storage/compute direction those form answers feed into is precisely the lever that keeps the *expensive* part cheap too.

-- D1 schema for Request-Access submissions.
-- Apply: npx wrangler d1 execute khelsutra-access --remote --file=schema.sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS access_requests (
  id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
  segment    TEXT,            -- 'academy' | 'individual'
  name       TEXT,
  email      TEXT NOT NULL,
  city       TEXT,
  org_name   TEXT,            -- academy / club name
  courts     TEXT,            -- '1' | '2-4' | '5+'
  has_gpu    TEXT,            -- 'yes' | 'unsure' | 'no' | 'prefer-cloud'
  storage    TEXT,            -- csv of gdrive,onedrive,dropbox,bucket,local,unsure
  message    TEXT,            -- free text
  consent    INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
  country    TEXT,            -- coarse, from cf-ipcountry (no raw IP stored)
  user_agent TEXT
);

### 475. assistant (2026-06-20T06:38:21.557Z)

// Storage adapters for Request-Access submissions.
//
// PORTABILITY (see docs/PORTABILITY.md): the business logic (validate / toRow /
// handleRequest in index.js) is runtime-agnostic — ALL database coupling lives here.
// The Cloudflare Worker uses d1Store(env.DB); an always-on VM uses sqliteStore(db)
// over node:sqlite (or better-sqlite3). Same schema.sql, same INSERT — zero dialect
// change. To move hosts, swap the adapter, not the logic.

export const INSERT_SQL =
  `INSERT INTO access_requests
     (segment, name, email, city, org_name, courts, has_gpu, storage, message, consent, country, user_agent)
   VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`;

export const insertArgs = (r) => [
  r.segment, r.name, r.email, r.city, r.org_name, r.courts,
  r.has_gpu, r.storage, r.message, r.consent, r.country, r.user_agent,
];

// Cloudflare D1 (async): prepare(sql).bind(...args).run()
export function d1Store(DB) {
  return {
    async insertAccessRequest(r) {
      await DB.prepare(INSERT_SQL).bind(...insertArgs(r)).run();
    },
  };
}

// node:sqlite / better-sqlite3 (sync): prepare(sql).run(...args). For the VM host.
export function sqliteStore(db) {
  const stmt = db.prepare(INSERT_SQL);
  return {
    async insertAccessRequest(r) {
      stmt.run(...insertArgs(r));
    },
  };
}

### 483. assistant (2026-06-20T06:38:50.786Z)

// Always-on VM host for the KhelSutra site — dependency-free (Node 22+ only).
// Serves public/ and POST /api/request-access into SQLite (node:sqlite), using the
// SAME handler logic as the Cloudflare Worker (src/index.js). This is the portability
// fallback "if Cloudflare acts out" — see docs/PORTABILITY.md.
//
//   PORT=8080 DB_PATH=/var/lib/khelsutra/access.db node server.js
//
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import { join, extname, normalize } from "node:path";
import { DatabaseSync } from "node:sqlite";
import { handleRequest } from "./src/index.js";
import { sqliteStore } from "./src/store.js";

const PORT = Number(process.env.PORT) || 8080;
const ROOT = import.meta.dirname;
const PUBLIC = join(ROOT, "public");
const DB_PATH = process.env.DB_PATH || join(ROOT, "access.db");

const db = new DatabaseSync(DB_PATH);
db.exec(await readFile(join(ROOT, "schema.sql"), "utf8")); // CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS — idempotent
const store = sqliteStore(db);

const TYPES = {
  ".html": "text/html; charset=utf-8", ".png": "image/png", ".svg": "image/svg+xml",
  ".css": "text/css", ".js": "text/javascript", ".ico": "image/x-icon", ".txt": "text/plain",
};

async function toRequest(req) {
  const url = `http://${req.headers.host || "localhost"}${req.url}`;
  let body;
  if (req.method !== "GET" && req.method !== "HEAD") {
    const chunks = [];
    for await (const c of req) chunks.push(c);
    body = Buffer.concat(chunks);
  }
  return new Request(url, { method: req.method, headers: req.headers, body });
}

async function send(res, response) {
  res.statusCode = response.status;
  for (const [k, v] of response.headers) res.setHeader(k, v);
  res.end(Buffer.from(await response.arrayBuffer()));
}

async function serveStatic(pathname, res) {
  const rel = pathname === "/" ? "index.html"
    : pathname === "/capture" ? "capture.html"
    : pathname.replace(/^\/+/, "");
  const file = normalize(join(PUBLIC, rel));
  if (!file.startsWith(PUBLIC)) { res.statusCode = 403; return res.end("forbidden"); }
  try {
    const data = await readFile(file);
    res.setHeader("content-type", TYPES[extname(file)] || "application/octet-stream");
    res.end(data);
  } catch {
    res.statusCode = 404;
    res.end("not found");
  }
}

createServer(async (req, res) => {
  try {
    const pathname = decodeURIComponent(new URL(`http://x${req.url}`).pathname);
    if (pathname === "/api/request-access") {
      if (req.method !== "POST") { res.statusCode = 405; return res.end('{"ok":false,"error":"method not allowed"}'); }
      return await send(res, await handleRequest(await toRequest(req), store));
    }
    return await serveStatic(pathname, res);
  } catch (err) {
    res.statusCode = 500;
    res.end('{"ok":false,"error":"server error"}');
    console.error(err);
  }
}).listen(PORT, () => console.log(`khelsutra site listening on :${PORT}  (sqlite: ${DB_PATH})`));

### 487. assistant (2026-06-20T06:39:23.754Z)

// Tests for the Request-Access logic + the storage adapters. Run: node --test src/
import { test } from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { DatabaseSync } from "node:sqlite";
import worker, { validate, toRow, isBot, handleRequest } from "./index.js";
import { d1Store, sqliteStore, insertArgs, INSERT_SQL } from "./store.js";

// A store spy (runtime-agnostic) — what handleRequest depends on.
function fakeStore() {
  const rows = [];
  return { rows, async insertAccessRequest(r) { rows.push(r); } };
}
// A D1-shaped fake (prepare().bind().run()) — for the Cloudflare adapter + fetch routing.
function fakeDB() {
  const inserts = [];
  return {
    inserts,
    prepare(sql) {
      return { bind(...args) { return { async run() { inserts.push({ sql, args }); } }; } };
    },
  };
}
function req(payload, headers = {}) {
  return new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json", ...headers },
    body: JSON.stringify(payload),
  });
}

test("validate requires a well-formed email", () => {
  assert.equal(validate({}).ok, false);
  assert.equal(validate({ email: "nope" }).ok, false);
  assert.equal(validate({ email: "a@b.co" }).ok, true);
});

test("isBot detects a filled honeypot only", () => {
  assert.equal(isBot({}), false);
  assert.equal(isBot({ company: "" }), false);
  assert.equal(isBot({ company: "spam" }), true);
});

test("toRow maps + sanitizes a valid payload", () => {
  const r = toRow(
    { segment: "academy", email: "a@b.co", storage: ["gdrive", "bucket", "evil"], has_gpu: "yes", courts: "2-4", consent: true },
    { country: "IN", userAgent: "x" }
  );
  assert.equal(r.segment, "academy");
  assert.equal(r.storage, "gdrive,bucket"); // unknown "evil" dropped
  assert.equal(r.has_gpu, "yes");
  assert.equal(r.courts, "2-4");
  assert.equal(r.consent, 1);
  assert.equal(r.country, "IN");
});

test("toRow rejects invalid enum values", () => {
  const r = toRow({ segment: "hacker", has_gpu: "maybe", courts: "9", consent: 0 });
  assert.equal(r.segment, null);
  assert.equal(r.has_gpu, null);
  assert.equal(r.courts, null);
  assert.equal(r.consent, 0);
  assert.equal(r.storage, "");
});

test("handleRequest inserts a valid submission via the store", async () => {
  const store = fakeStore();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "a@b.co", segment: "individual", storage: ["gdrive"] }), store);
  assert.equal(res.status, 200);
  assert.deepEqual(await res.json(), { ok: true });
  assert.equal(store.rows.length, 1);
  assert.equal(store.rows[0].email, "a@b.co");
});

test("handleRequest drops a bot without inserting", async () => {
  const store = fakeStore();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "a@b.co", company: "spam" }), store);
  assert.equal(res.status, 200);
  assert.equal(store.rows.length, 0);
});

test("handleRequest rejects a bad email with 400", async () => {
  const store = fakeStore();
  const res = await handleRequest(req({ email: "nope" }), store);
  assert.equal(res.status, 400);
  assert.equal(store.rows.length, 0);
});

test("handleRequest rejects invalid JSON with 400", async () => {
  const store = fakeStore();
  const bad = new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access", {
    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: "{not json",
  });
  const res = await handleRequest(bad, store);
  assert.equal(res.status, 400);
  assert.equal(store.rows.length, 0);
});

test("d1Store issues the bound INSERT (Cloudflare path)", async () => {
  const db = fakeDB();
  await d1Store(db).insertAccessRequest(toRow({ email: "a@b.co", segment: "individual" }));
  assert.equal(db.inserts.length, 1);
  assert.equal(db.inserts[0].sql, INSERT_SQL);
  assert.equal(db.inserts[0].args[2], "a@b.co"); // email is the 3rd column
});

test("sqliteStore persists a real row (node:sqlite round-trip — the VM path)", async () => {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(":memory:");
  db.exec(`CREATE TABLE access_requests (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, created_at TEXT,
    segment TEXT, name TEXT, email TEXT, city TEXT, org_name TEXT, courts TEXT, has_gpu TEXT,
    storage TEXT, message TEXT, consent INTEGER, country TEXT, user_agent TEXT)`);
  const store = sqliteStore(db);
  await store.insertAccessRequest(toRow(
    { segment: "academy", email: "coach@club.in", storage: ["gdrive", "bucket"], has_gpu: "yes", consent: true },
    { country: "IN" }
  ));
  const got = db.prepare("SELECT email, segment, has_gpu, storage, consent, country FROM access_requests").get();
  assert.equal(got.email, "coach@club.in");
  assert.equal(got.segment, "academy");
  assert.equal(got.has_gpu, "yes");
  assert.equal(got.storage, "gdrive,bucket");
  assert.equal(got.consent, 1);
  assert.equal(got.country, "IN");
});

test("fetch routes: POST /api hits the handler, other paths delegate to ASSETS", async () => {
  const apiRes = await worker.fetch(req({ email: "a@b.co" }), { DB: fakeDB() });
  assert.equal(apiRes.status, 200);

  let assetCalled = false;
  const env = { DB: fakeDB(), ASSETS: { fetch: async () => { assetCalled = true; return new Response("ok"); } } };
  const assetRes = await worker.fetch(new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/"), env);
  assert.equal(assetCalled, true);
  assert.equal(await assetRes.text(), "ok");
});

test("fetch rejects non-POST to the API with 405", async () => {
  const res = await worker.fetch(new Request("https://khelsutra.guru/api/request-access"), { DB: fakeDB() });
  assert.equal(res.status, 405);
});

### 494. assistant (2026-06-20T06:41:38.250Z)

# Portability — Cloudflare now, always-on VM if needed

> **Status:** design + proven (2026-06-20). **Decision:** keep the site/form host-portable so
> moving off Cloudflare to an owner-run always-on VM ("if Cloudflare acts out") is an **adapter
> swap, not a rewrite.**

## The shape that makes it portable
- **Logic is runtime-agnostic.** `src/index.js` uses Web-standard `Request`/`Response` and pure
  helpers (`validate`, `toRow`, `isBot`, `handleRequest`) — no host-specific calls.
- **All DB coupling is in adapters** (`src/store.js`): `d1Store` (Cloudflare D1) and `sqliteStore`
  (node:sqlite / better-sqlite3). `handleRequest(request, store)` takes the store by injection.
- **One schema, one SQL.** `schema.sql` and the `INSERT` are plain SQLite, so D1 and a VM's SQLite
  file are byte-for-byte compatible — zero dialect change. (Postgres later = minor placeholder tweaks.)

## Two hosts, same code
| | Cloudflare (now) | Always-on VM (fallback) |
|---|---|---|
| Entry | `src/index.js` (Worker `fetch`) | `server.js` (`node:http`) |
| Static | `env.ASSETS` | `server.js` serves `public/` |
| Storage | `d1Store(env.DB)` → D1 | `sqliteStore(db)` → `node:sqlite` file |
| Country | `cf-ipcountry` header | optional (GeoIP / null) |
| Deploy | git → Workers Builds | `node server.js` under systemd |
| TLS/DNS | Cloudflare | keep Cloudflare in front, **or** Caddy/Let's Encrypt |

**Proven, not asserted:** `node --test src/` is 12 green incl. a real `node:sqlite` round-trip, and
`server.js` was smoke-tested locally — it served the form and persisted a real submission to SQLite.

## VM deploy runbook ("if Cloudflare acts out")
Node 22+ only — **no `npm install`** (node:sqlite is built in).
```
# on the VM:
git clone … && cd khelsutra/site        # or scp the site/ folder
PORT=8080 DB_PATH=/var/lib/khelsutra/access.db node server.js
# put it under systemd (auto-restart) + a reverse proxy (Caddy/nginx) for TLS,
# then point DNS at the VM. ~1 hour; logic + schema unchanged.
```

## Honest notes
- "Cloudflare acts out" usually means **Workers/D1 pricing or limits, not DNS** — so the cheapest
  move is to keep Cloudflare DNS/CDN in front and shift only the **compute** to the VM (point a
  route/origin at the box). Full exit (own DNS + Caddy TLS) is also supported.
- You can run **both** (Cloudflare primary + VM warm standby) since the schema is identical; a
  periodic `access_requests` export keeps them in sync.
- `server.js` is intentionally dependency-free; if you later want Postgres or a framework (Hono runs
  on Workers *and* Node), it slots in behind the same `store` + handler.

### 519. assistant (2026-06-20T07:08:50.890Z)

// [VM/droplet host harness] Boots the REAL server.js (node:http + node:sqlite) and
// round-trips it over HTTP — the same proof we run on the droplet, automated. Keeps the
// repo compatible with BOTH hosts: src/index.test.js covers the Cloudflare (Worker+D1)
// path; this covers the Node+SQLite path. Run: node --test src/   (or: npm run test:vm)
import { test, before, after } from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { createServer } from "node:net";
import { mkdtempSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { DatabaseSync } from "node:sqlite";

function freePort() {
  return new Promise((res) => {
    const s = createServer();
    s.listen(0, "127.0.0.1", () => { const p = s.address().port; s.close(() => res(p)); });
  });
}
async function waitUp(url, ms = 10000) {
  const end = Date.now() + ms;
  while (Date.now() < end) {
    try { await fetch(url); return; } catch { await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 150)); }
  }
  throw new Error("server.js did not start listening in time");
}

let proc, base, dbPath, dir;

before(async () => {
  const port = await freePort();
  dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "ks-vm-"));
  dbPath = join(dir, "access.db");
  base = `http://127.0.0.1:${port}`;
  const serverPath = join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "server.js");
  proc = spawn(process.execPath, [serverPath], {
    env: { ...process.env, PORT: String(port), DB_PATH: dbPath },
    stdio: "ignore",
  });
  await waitUp(`${base}/`);
});

after(() => {
  proc?.kill();
  try { rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); } catch {}
});

test("[VM host] server.js serves the form", async () => {
  const html = await (await fetch(`${base}/`)).text();
  assert.match(html, /id="af"/);
  assert.match(html, /Get into the KhelSutra alpha/);
});

test("[VM host] serves /capture and /og.png", async () => {
  assert.equal((await fetch(`${base}/capture`)).status, 200);
  assert.equal((await fetch(`${base}/og.png`)).status, 200);
});

test("[VM host] POST /api/request-access persists to SQLite", async () => {
  const res = await fetch(`${base}/api/request-access`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ segment: "academy", email: "harness@khelsutra.guru", has_gpu: "yes", storage: ["gdrive", "bucket"], consent: true }),
  });
  assert.equal(res.status, 200);
  assert.deepEqual(await res.json(), { ok: true });

  const db = new DatabaseSync(dbPath);
  const row = db.prepare("SELECT email, segment, has_gpu, storage, consent FROM access_requests").get();
  assert.equal(row.email, "harness@khelsutra.guru");
  assert.equal(row.segment, "academy");
  assert.equal(row.has_gpu, "yes");
  assert.equal(row.storage, "gdrive,bucket");
  assert.equal(row.consent, 1);
});

test("[VM host] honeypot + bad email are rejected without persisting", async () => {
  const bot = await fetch(`${base}/api/request-access`, {
    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ email: "x@y.co", company: "spam" }),
  });
  assert.equal(bot.status, 200); // dropped silently
  const bad = await fetch(`${base}/api/request-access`, {
    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ email: "nope" }),
  });
  assert.equal(bad.status, 400);

  const db = new DatabaseSync(dbPath);
  const { n } = db.prepare("SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM access_requests").get();
  assert.equal(n, 1); // only the valid submission above
});

test("[VM host] GET /api/request-access -> 405", async () => {
  assert.equal((await fetch(`${base}/api/request-access`)).status, 405);
});

### 522. assistant (2026-06-20T07:10:22.154Z)

name: test
on:
  push:
    branches: [master]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  site:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: site
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: "24" # node:sqlite is unflagged; server.test.js boots the VM host
      # No deps to install (the site is dependency-free). This runs BOTH paths:
      #   src/index.test.js  -> Cloudflare Worker + D1 logic
      #   src/server.test.js -> boots server.js (node:http + node:sqlite) and round-trips it
      - run: node --test src/

### 524. assistant (2026-06-20T07:10:42.695Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Live VM smoke test: copy site/ to a droplet, boot server.js, round-trip it, report.
# Proves the repo's Node + SQLite path on a REAL VM (the Cloudflare path is covered by
# `npm test`). Idempotent; cleans up its temp DB. Needs Node >= 22 on the VM (or a
# self-contained binary at $REMOTE_DIR/node/bin/node).
#
#   SSH_TARGET=root@1.2.3.4 [SSH_KEY=~/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet] \
#   [REMOTE_DIR=/srv/khelsutra-site] [PORT=8787] scripts/droplet-smoke.sh
set -euo pipefail
: "${SSH_TARGET:?set SSH_TARGET=user@host}"
KEY="${SSH_KEY:-$HOME/.ssh/khelsutra_droplet}"
DIR="${REMOTE_DIR:-/srv/khelsutra-site}"
PORT="${PORT:-8787}"
SITE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)" # the site/ folder (script lives in site/scripts/)
SSHO=(-i "$KEY" -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new)

echo "→ scp $SITE  →  $SSH_TARGET:$DIR/site"
ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" "mkdir -p '$DIR'"
scp "${SSHO[@]}" -qr "$SITE" "$SSH_TARGET:$DIR/"

ssh "${SSHO[@]}" "$SSH_TARGET" "bash -s -- '$DIR' '$PORT'" <<'REMOTE'
set -e
DIR="$1"; PORT="$2"
NODE="$( [ -x "$DIR/node/bin/node" ] && echo "$DIR/node/bin/node" || command -v node || true )"
[ -n "$NODE" ] || { echo "FAIL: no Node (>=22) on the VM"; exit 1; }
cd "$DIR/site"
DB="$DIR/smoke.db"; rm -f "$DB"
PORT="$PORT" DB_PATH="$DB" nohup "$NODE" server.js > "$DIR/smoke.log" 2>&1 &
SRV=$!
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do curl -sf -o /dev/null "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/" && break || sleep 1; done
form=$(curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/" | grep -c 'id="af"' || true)
post=$(curl -s -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"smoke@khelsutra.guru","segment":"individual","has_gpu":"yes","storage":["gdrive"],"consent":true}' \
  "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/api/request-access")
row=$("$NODE" --input-type=module -e 'import {DatabaseSync} from "node:sqlite"; const r=new DatabaseSync(process.argv[1]).prepare("SELECT email FROM access_requests").get(); console.log(r?r.email:"none")' "$DB")
kill "$SRV" 2>/dev/null || true
rm -f "$DB" "$DIR/smoke.log"
echo "form-marker:$form  api:$post  db-row:$row"

### 548. assistant (2026-06-20T07:20:53.175Z)

// Notification adapters (best-effort — a notify failure must never fail a submission).
// Cloudflare path: Email Routing's send_email binding -> a VERIFIED destination (free).
// VM path: no-op for now (add SMTP later — see docs/PORTABILITY.md).

export function buildSubmissionEmail(r) {
  const text = [
    `Segment: ${r.segment || "?"}`,
    `Name:    ${r.name || ""}`,
    `Email:   ${r.email || ""}`,
    `City:    ${r.city || ""}`,
    r.org_name ? `Academy: ${r.org_name} (${r.courts || "?"} courts)` : null,
    `GPU:     ${r.has_gpu || ""}`,
    `Storage: ${r.storage || ""}`,
    `Consent: ${r.consent ? "yes" : "no"}`,
    r.message ? `\nMessage:\n${r.message}` : null,
  ].filter(Boolean).join("\n");
  const subject = `New KhelSutra alpha request — ${r.segment || "?"} · ${r.email || "?"}`;
  return { subject, text };
}

// Cloudflare Email Routing send binding (`[[send_email]]` in wrangler.toml).
// `to` must be a verified destination address. Dynamically imports cloudflare:email so
// this module stays loadable under Node (the VM path / tests never call into it).
export function cfEmailNotify(binding, from, to) {
  return {
    async notify(r) {
      if (!binding || !from || !to) return;
      const { subject, text } = buildSubmissionEmail(r);
      const raw =
        `From: KhelSutra <${from}>\r\nTo: ${to}\r\nSubject: ${subject}\r\n` +
        `MIME-Version: 1.0\r\nContent-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8\r\n\r\n${text}`;
      const { EmailMessage } = await import("cloudflare:email");
      await binding.send(new EmailMessage(from, to, raw));
    },
  };
}

export function noopNotify() {
  return { async notify() {} };
}

### 593. assistant (2026-06-20T07:34:42.036Z)

**Verified, diagnosed, fixed, and now in sync** ✅ — both serve **md5 `1598a01a9810`**, byte-identical.

**What differed:** *line endings only* — the droplet had **CRLF**, Cloudflare had **LF**. Identical content; they render the same. **Root cause:** the droplet was `scp`'d from the Windows working copy (git's `autocrlf` makes it CRLF), while Cloudflare deploys straight from git (LF). I normalized the droplet to LF → they now match exactly.

**Why the tests didn't catch it:** the harness boots `server.js` and checks the form *works* on each host — it tests **code/behavior**, not **byte-parity between the two live deployments**. And CRLF vs LF changes neither rendering nor the `id="af"` check, so a content test passes for both. The drift was a *transport* artifact (scp from Windows), invisible to a code test. Fix = make line endings deterministic + add a live-parity check. Doing both now:
